This event is significant because it marked a shift from a life of fear and uncertainty to one of relative stability. I admire Olive's strength and determination, as they are qualities I strive to embody in my own life. This composition will explore one such instance of Olive's resilience and how it impacted me as a reader.\n\nQuotation Analysis:\n\"I have not been forgotten, then. I am still remembered in the world I left behind\" (Mifflin 132). This revelation occurs when Olive learns that her siblings have not forgotten her and have been actively seeking her whereabouts. Here's a more detailed explanation of the sources of international commercial law you mentioned:\n\n1. **International Treaties**: These are formal agreements between states that create legally binding obligations. In the context of international commercial law, treaties can cover a wide range of topics, such as trade, investment, intellectual property, and dispute resolution. Examples include the World Trade Organization (WTO) agreements, the United Nations Convention on Contracts for the International Sale of Goods (CISG), and various bilateral and regional free trade agreements.\n\n2. **Supranational Rules**: These are rules that apply to multiple states and are created by international organizations with supranational authority. The European Union (EU) is a prime example, with its extensive body of law that applies to all member states. EU law covers many areas relevant to international commerce, such as competition law, consumer protection, and data privacy.\n\n3. **International Customs**: These are practices that are widely accepted and followed by businesses and traders in international commerce. While not formally codified, these customs can be recognized as law by courts and tribunals. For example, the Incoterms rules, published by the International Chamber of Commerce, are widely used to clarify the responsibilities of buyers and sellers in international sales contracts.\n\n4. **Lex Mercatoria**: This term refers to a body of transnational commercial law that has developed through the practices and customs of international traders, rather than through formal legislation or treaties. It includes principles such as good faith, fair dealing, and the autonomy of the parties. While not a formal source of law, lex mercatoria is often recognized and applied by international commercial arbitrators and courts.\n\nIt's worth noting that these sources of international commercial law often interact and overlap with each other, and with domestic laws. Therefore, understanding the interplay between these sources is crucial for anyone involved in international commerce.",
